# Evydence 9/10 Production And Open Source Backlog

Repository: `https://github.com/aatuh/evydence`

Baseline branch: `master`

Baseline commit:
`e0179af0ed65e9b001996c6fd678da71f201e74d`

Baseline commit date: `2026-08-07T17:31:25+03:00`

Baseline commit message:
`feat(storage): reconcile staged and orphaned payloads (EVY-403)`

Baseline reconciliation (2026-10-06): the commit above was the verified local
`master` head at that time, not a live default-branch snapshot. Intervening
commits from the original audit baseline
`187d28f4419c20e61cc6ddceaf5bbe7bea7ed9b0` were inspected; their
implementation/tracking records remain in the tickets below. Subsequent work
proceeded on `agent/evy-403-completion`.

Integration checkpoint (2026-10-12): [PR #27](https://github.com/aatuh/evydence/pull/27)
merged that feature branch into `master` at
`546de837479e7123870ccec0d8cb0811a00f312d`, preserving all 631 feature commits.
The merged tree matches validated feature head
`6abac9670e566375256dbb44a4126a6822409da4`. Required Production Check and
CodeQL Analyze passed on that feature head; post-merge CI is separate evidence
and must be checked before claiming its result. See [implementation handoff](HANDOFF.md)
for the takeover snapshot and portable goal. Per-ticket completion records
remain authoritative; this integration changes no ticket checkmarks.

This backlog is the execution handoff for taking Evydence from a useful
production-oriented prerelease to a credible 9/10 project across dependency
worthiness, architecture, tests, documentation, open-source trust, ecosystem
fit, feature completeness, and API design.

It is intentionally strict. Passing repository-local checks is necessary but
is not sufficient for the external-review, repository-settings, and real-user
evidence tickets. Codex must not fabricate external evidence or mark those
tickets complete without verifiable proof.

## Status Syntax

- `[ ]` means incomplete.
- `[x]` means complete and fully verified.
- An epic may be changed to `[x]` only after every child ticket is `[x]` and
  the epic exit criteria pass.
- A ticket may be changed to `[x]` only after all implementation steps,
  acceptance criteria, validation commands, documentation updates, and
  migration or compatibility obligations are complete.
- Partial implementation does not justify `[x]`.
- Blocked tickets stay `[ ]` and receive a short `Blocked:` note with the
  exact missing evidence or prerequisite.

## Codex Operating Contract

1. Revalidate the repository head before starting. If `master` no longer
   points to the baseline commit, inspect the intervening commits, update the
   baseline section in this file, and reconcile completed or obsolete work.
   Never assume the backlog still matches changed code.

2. Work in dependency order unless a ticket explicitly has no dependency.
   Do not start a ticket while a listed dependency is incomplete.

3. Work on one ticket at a time. Keep the working tree clean between
   tickets. Do not combine unrelated tickets.

4. Use Conventional Commits for every commit:

   ```text
   <type>(<scope>): <imperative summary> (<ticket-id>)
   ```

   Allowed types:

   ```text
   feat
   fix
   refactor
   perf
   test
   docs
   build
   ci
   chore
   revert
   ```

   Examples:

   ```text
   fix(verification): reject metadata-only cosign checks (EVY-101)
   refactor(persistence): add transaction unit of work (EVY-203)
   test(idempotency): cover concurrent duplicate requests (EVY-303)
   docs(api): document endpoint stability levels (EVY-701)
   ci(release): enforce changelog and API diff gates (EVY-1408)
   ```

   Use `!` and a `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer for intentional breaking changes.
   Breaking changes must also include migration notes, OpenAPI updates, SDK
   updates, examples, and changelog entries in the same ticket.

5. Commit after every completed ticket or meaningful, independently valid
   subtask. Do not create vague `WIP` commits. Every intermediate commit must
   build and pass the relevant focused tests. If a ticket is too large for
   one coherent commit, split it into explicit sub-tickets in this backlog
   before implementing it.

6. The final implementation commit for a ticket must update the ticket
   checkbox to `[x]`. Add a `Completion evidence:` line below the ticket with
   the exact checks run. Record the implementation commit SHA in the repository
   tracking record after that commit exists; a commit cannot contain its own
   final SHA. If the ticket used several meaningful commits, list all of them.

7. Preserve existing code and comments outside the explicitly changed area.
   Use ASCII characters in comments and documentation. Keep Go code formatted
   with `gofmt`, keep code lines near 80 characters where practical, and
   avoid clever abstractions without a measured need.

8. Never weaken validation, tenant isolation, append-only history, safe error
   handling, redaction, or non-claim language merely to make a test pass.

9. Do not add claims that Evydence proves legal compliance, certification,
   complete SBOM coverage, authoritative vulnerability truth, regulator
   acceptance, auditor acceptance, or guaranteed release security.

10. Do not add dependencies casually. For each new dependency, record:
    maintainer activity, license, release cadence, vulnerability history,
    transitive footprint, why the standard library is insufficient, and the
    fallback or replacement strategy.

11. Never store or commit credentials, customer data, raw private evidence,
    signing keys, database URLs, access tokens, package secrets, or provider
    credentials. Use sanitized fixtures.

12. If an external or maintainer-owned ticket cannot be completed by Codex,
    Codex may prepare scripts, templates, checklists, and evidence locations,
    but must leave the ticket `[ ]` until the named owner supplies proof.

## Required Commit Body

For non-trivial commits, include:

```text
Why:
- <problem and user impact>

What:
- <implementation summary>

Verification:
- <commands and results>

Compatibility:
- <API, data, migration, or deployment impact>

Ticket: <ticket-id>
```

## Validation Matrix

Run the narrowest useful checks during development and the full required gate
before checking the ticket complete.

| Change type               | Minimum required checks                                                                     |
|---------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Documentation only        | `make docs-check`                                                                           |
| Go implementation         | `go test ./...`, `make fast-check`                                                          |
| HTTP or OpenAPI           | `make openapi-check`, `make sdk-check`, route tests                                         |
| Persistence or migrations | PostgreSQL integration tests, `make migration-compatibility-check`, `make production-check` |
| Concurrency               | focused stress test, `go test -race ./...`, `make production-check`                         |
| Parser or archive         | fixture corpus, fuzz seeds, negative tests, `make production-check`                         |
| Security or cryptography  | focused vectors, `make release-check`, `gosec`, `govulncheck`, CodeQL-compatible tests      |
| Deployment                | Compose and Helm checks, black-box binary or image test                                     |
| Release                   | every command in `docs/reference/stable-v0.1.0-exit-criteria.md`                            |

If a required dependency such as PostgreSQL, MinIO, Docker, or release signing
material is unavailable, do not mark the ticket complete. Record the blocker.

## Ticket Definition Of Done

Every ticket is done only when:

- the implementation is complete and intentionally scoped;
- positive, negative, boundary, and failure-path tests exist;
- race-sensitive behavior has race or stress coverage;
- public API, OpenAPI, SDKs, examples, migrations, and docs are aligned;
- user-visible changes appear in `CHANGELOG.md`;
- no generated file is stale;
- relevant local and CI-equivalent checks pass;
- the completion commit uses Conventional Commits;
- the ticket checkbox is `[x]`;
- completion evidence is recorded;
- the working tree is clean.

## Epic Definition Of Done

Every epic is done only when:

- all child tickets are `[x]`;
- the epic exit criteria pass;
- no open P0 or P1 defect remains in the epic area;
- the objective scorecard is updated with repository evidence;
- documentation states remaining limitations honestly;
- the final epic commit is conventional and records the validation evidence.

## Target 9/10 Evidence Gates

| Dimension             | Evidence required before claiming 9/10                                                                                                                                 |
|-----------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Dependency-worthiness | Stable supported core, atomic writes, safe concurrency, typed clients, upgrade evidence, published support window                                                      |
| Code and architecture | No process-wide god aggregate as the production transaction boundary, explicit bounded contexts, database-authoritative writes, enforced dependency direction          |
| Tests                 | Failure atomicity, concurrency, race, fuzz, standards corpus, live PostgreSQL and object-store tests, released-version upgrades, black-box release tests               |
| Documentation         | Five-minute verified path, accurate API and compatibility docs, operator runbooks, troubleshooting, migration guides, honest limitations                               |
| Open-source trust     | Protected releases, branch and review controls, security intake, provenance, external security review, at least two active maintainers or a documented continuity plan |
| Ecosystem fit         | Standards-conformant ingestion, real integrations, clear alternatives, design-partner evidence, narrow differentiated scope                                            |
| Feature completeness  | Core release decision and customer package workflow complete end to end; peripheral features explicitly experimental or removed                                        |
| API design            | Small stable core, typed errors, pagination, concurrency controls, deprecation policy, compatibility gate, published SDKs                                              |

## Execution Order

```text
EVY-00
  -> EVY-10
  -> EVY-20, EVY-30, EVY-40
  -> EVY-50, EVY-60
  -> EVY-70, EVY-80, EVY-90, EVY-100
  -> EVY-110, EVY-120, EVY-130
  -> EVY-140, EVY-150, EVY-160
  -> EVY-170
```

Do not cut a stable release before EVY-170 explicitly permits it.

## Existing Foundation To Preserve

The backlog starts from substantial existing work. Do not rebuild or
discard these without a ticket-specific reason:

- Go service, worker, migration CLI, and helper CLI.
- PostgreSQL, filesystem, and S3-compatible adapters.
- `/v1` OpenAPI generation and route-contract checks.
- Tenant-scoped authentication and authorization concepts.
- Append-only evidence, decision, audit, bundle, and package concepts.
- Release candidate artifacts, checksums, signed manifest machinery,
  CodeQL, OpenSSF Scorecard, `gosec`, `govulncheck`, lint, race, and
  coverage gates.
- Compose, production-like Compose, Helm, air-gap, observability, backup,
  upgrade, and release documentation.
- Customer CVE review and end-to-end evidence examples.
- Existing negative archive verification tests, including duplicate JSON
  key handling.

Improve these incrementally. Do not replace proven behavior with a broad
rewrite that lacks migration, compatibility, and test evidence.

## Baseline Blockers This Backlog Must Close

These are known at the baseline commit and should be treated as concrete
starting evidence, not speculative concerns:

1. `internal/app/integrity_runtime.go` can label metadata-presence checks
   as successful Cosign verification without cryptographic verification.
2. Object-retention verification can report a positive status without a
   provider-backed verifier.
3. `internal/app/ledger.go` and related services commonly mutate process
   maps before durable persistence succeeds.
4. `internal/app/ledger.go` uses check-then-act idempotency that permits
   concurrent duplicate side effects.
5. `internal/app/state.go` can silently normalize to empty state when a
   clone marshal or unmarshal fails.
6. Object bytes can be written before database metadata commits, without
   a complete staged/finalized reconciliation protocol.
7. Existing parsers model reduced subsets and can reject valid standard
   extension fields.
8. HTTP request buffering and application upload limits are inconsistent.
9. `sdk/go/evydence/client.go` is an unpublished, partial, untyped wrapper
   with no production constructor or default timeout.
10. SDK fields including release `project_id` and artifact `release_id`
    do not match fields consumed by current server handlers.
11. `Ledger` is a process-wide mutable aggregate with a global mutex and
    broad resource ownership.
12. Readiness reports configured interfaces rather than live dependency
    health, and `/v1/version` reports `dev`.
13. `CHANGELOG.md` and current public release truth are stale relative to
    `master`.
14. Governance, release authority, security response, and continuity
    remain concentrated in one maintainer account.

## [x] EPIC EVY-00: Program Controls, Baseline, And Scope Freeze

**Objective:** Create a repository-local operating contract, objective scorecard, accurate release truth, and explicit product/API boundaries before large technical changes begin.

**Score impact:** All dimensions. This epic prevents untracked work, fabricated completion, stale release claims, and accidental compatibility debt.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- The repository contains a current `AGENTS.md` and this backlog.
- Every ticket can be mapped to a GitHub issue or milestone.
- The scorecard measures repository and external evidence separately.
- The supported product wedge and API stability classes are explicit.
- README, changelog, release metadata, and published release state agree.

## [x] EPIC EVY-10: Trust Semantics And Immediate Correctness Blockers

**Objective:** Remove misleading success states and make verification, readiness, audit-chain, and version responses mean exactly what their names claim.

**Score impact:** Dependency-worthiness, security trust, API design, documentation, and feature completeness.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- No metadata-presence check can return a cryptographic `passed` result.
- Verification results use a documented taxonomy.
- Audit-chain verification recomputes content, not only stored hashes.
- Readiness performs live dependency checks.
- Version responses identify the exact binary and release evidence.

### [x] EVY-101: Stop metadata-only Cosign checks from returning passed

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `M`
- Depends on: `None`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/app/integrity_runtime.go`
- `internal/adapters/httpapi/openapi_operations.go`
- `docs/api.md`
- `docs/architecture.md`
- `CHANGELOG.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Change `VerifyCosignSignature` so the current metadata-only logic returns `not_verified` or `limited`, never `passed`.
2. Rename internal checks to describe what was actually assessed: digest binding, signature material present, and Rekor metadata present.
3. Either rename the route to a metadata-assessment operation or retain it only as deprecated while EVY-602 implements real verification.
4. Add a stable Problem Details code when callers request full verification but no verifier or trust policy is configured.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- An arbitrary non-empty signature string can never produce `passed`.
- Docs and OpenAPI explicitly state the temporary limitation.
- Existing clients receive a safe migration path.
- Negative tests prove false material is not accepted.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/httpapi`
- `make openapi-check`
- `make release-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
fix(verification): reject metadata-only cosign success (EVY-101)
```

**Completion evidence:** Implemented in `6f26bd79ab858962fd5bd722188d2cf960714e9f`. A metadata-only Cosign assessment now returns `limited` or `failed`, the retained route is OpenAPI-deprecated, and `require_full_verification` returns `COSIGN_FULL_VERIFICATION_UNAVAILABLE` when no verifier or trust policy is configured. Negative app and HTTP tests cover arbitrary non-empty signature material. `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/httpapi`, `make openapi-check`, `make docs-check`, and `make release-check` passed on 2026-07-22. No external verifier or compatibility waiver was claimed.

### [x] EVY-102: Define a verification result and assurance taxonomy

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `M`
- Depends on: `EVY-101`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/domain/`
- `internal/app/errors.go`
- `openapi.yaml`
- `docs/explanation/trust-model.md`
- `docs/reference/verification-results.md (new)`

**Implementation:**
1. Define machine-stable states such as `passed`, `failed`, `not_verified`, `limited`, `skipped`, and `error`.
2. Define an assurance level or verification profile that records exactly which trust material, identity policy, transparency proof, and payload were evaluated.
3. Prohibit `passed` when any required check is `warning`, `skipped`, or absent for the selected profile.
4. Version the result schema and add a migration for persisted legacy values.
5. Map old ambiguous records conservatively to `limited`.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- All verification APIs use the same state machine.
- Result aggregation is deterministic and tested.
- Persisted legacy results retain history without overstating assurance.
- Customer packages include profile and limitations.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/domain ./internal/app`
- `make migration-compatibility-check`
- `make openapi-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
feat(verification): add assurance result taxonomy (EVY-102)
```

**Completion evidence:** Implemented in `9cc054974c53794ab83cf74302a13344f0d8516f`. The shared, deterministic taxonomy has `passed`, `failed`, `not_verified`, `limited`, `skipped`, and `error` states; `passed` requires every profile-required check to pass. Generic, Cosign, provider, Merkle, backup, DSSE, and SSO verification records now persist versioned assurance profiles, limitations, and result schemas. Customer packages export release-scoped result/profile summaries without raw trust material. Migration `20260722000100_verification_assurance_taxonomy` preserves historical rows and maps profile-less ambiguous `passed` records to `limited`; the committed PostgreSQL regression test also asserts that stored failures remain failures. `go test ./internal/domain ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/httpapi ./internal/adapters/postgres -count=1`, `make openapi-check`, `make docs-check`, `make sdk-check`, `make lint`, `make gosec`, `make vuln`, and `make release-check` passed on 2026-07-22. `make migration-compatibility-check` passed, but its database-backed prefix/data migration scenarios were skipped locally because `EVYDENCE_TEST_DATABASE_URL` was not configured; no live migration execution is claimed.

### [x] EVY-103: Correct object-retention verification semantics

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `M`
- Depends on: `EVY-102`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/app/integrity_runtime.go`
- `internal/adapters/objectstore/s3/`
- `openapi.yaml`
- `docs/reference/external-controls-matrix.md`
- `docs/runbooks/object-store-recovery.md`

**Implementation:**
1. When no provider verifier is configured, return `not_verified`, not `verified`.
2. Separate recorded retention intent, bucket configuration evidence, sample-object evidence, and provider-enforced proof into distinct checks.
3. Require every positive provider result to identify bucket, object key if applicable, mode, duration, legal-hold state, provider, observation time, and limitations.
4. Add stale-evidence handling so old checks cannot remain implicitly current forever.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- No local-only record is labeled provider verified.
- A failed or unavailable provider check is distinguishable.
- Evidence age is visible and policy-configurable.
- S3/MinIO positive and negative fixtures cover versioning and lock modes.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/objectstore/s3`
- `make openapi-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
fix(retention): make provider verification truthful (EVY-103)
```

**Completion evidence:** Implemented in `13f5b536363f253f34673aed13245d396c2cefca` and `e14c7253c08876a40947be8c2854cfefe192bf44`. Local retention intent now records `not_verified` without a provider observation; provider observations persist provider, bucket, mode, duration, applicable sample-object/legal-hold state, observation/expiry time, bounded age, checks, and limitations. Provider failure and unavailability remain distinct, legacy records are conservatively migrated to `not_verified`, S3/MinIO checks require versioning, lock mode/duration, sample-object evidence, and observed legal-hold state, and stale observations no longer count as current in custody reports or customer-safe proof exports. - Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`

- Changing any covered field causes verification failure.
- Concurrent appends cannot reuse or skip a committed sequence.
- Checkpoint verification detects truncation and rewritten history after the checkpoint.
- Legacy chains remain verifiable through an explicit versioned path.

- `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/postgres`
- `go test -race ./...`
- `make production-check`


**Completion evidence:** Implemented in `7d23dbae143b743d641a164c4033f745fa1f7c69`. Verification now recomputes versioned canonical entry hashes and checks tenant ownership, schema version, sequence, previous hash, entry hash, and referenced local signatures without trusting stored canonical hashes. New v2 entries canonicalize timestamps at PostgreSQL microsecond precision and include all persisted entry fields; the legacy v1 path preserves its documented field set and deterministically handles PostgreSQL's lost sub-microsecond timestamp precision. PostgreSQL persists the v2 request/idempotency fields and serializes each tenant chain with a transaction advisory lock, reloads its durable head, allocates contiguous committed sequences, recomputes hashes, and relies on the existing `(tenant_id, sequence)` uniqueness constraint. The live regression test races two independent stores and asserts that the committed chain is exactly contiguous. Signed Merkle and release-manifest checkpoints are verifiable through generic subject types `audit_chain_checkpoint` and `audit_chain_release_manifest`; both truncation paths and changed covered fields are tested. ## [x] EPIC EVY-20: Atomic Persistence And Database-Authoritative State

**Objective:** Make every successful response correspond to a committed durable transaction and ensure every failed transaction leaves no visible domain mutation.

**Score impact:** Dependency-worthiness, code architecture, test trust, concurrency, resource management, and production stability.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Production writes are transaction-first and database-authoritative.
- A persistence failure cannot leak state into subsequent reads or writes.
- Silent serialization fallback is impossible.
- Production has no broad full-state mutation path.
- The in-process compatibility store is isolated to explicit local/test use.


**Completion evidence:** Implemented in `9550e4b`. PostgreSQL now reserves the tenant-, actor-, method-, path-, and key-scoped row before command execution; it uses conditional owner-hash leases, row locking, recovery after an expired lease, and owner-conditional completion/failure transitions. - Owner: `Codex`

- One idempotency key cannot create duplicate domain effects.
- Conflicting request content always returns the stable conflict code.
- Replay does not expose secrets to a different actor.


**Completion evidence:** Implemented in `9982e19`. A durable idempotent command now holds coordinated ownership while it reserves the key, executes against transaction-scoped repositories, appends audit/outbox effects, stores a safe terminal replay envelope, and commits; an error rolls back domain effects before a separate response-free failed state is recorded. The HTTP mutation wrapper runs handlers on an isolated command ledger and publishes cache state only after commit; durable deployments reload the authoritative state so worker updates cannot be overwritten. Release-bundle creation and subject verification now also use the active UoW path, including the tenant audit-chain verification case. Recursive redaction removes one-time credential and token-like fields from stored/replayed responses, and migration `20260726000300` removes known legacy response secrets. - Cross-tenant and cross-actor keys never collide.
- Abandoned reservations follow the documented recovery rule.

- `make production-check`


## [x] EPIC EVY-40: Object Storage Integrity, Streaming, And Recovery

**Objective:** Make object payload ingestion, metadata, reconciliation, backup, and recovery explicit across PostgreSQL and filesystem or S3-compatible storage.

**Score impact:** Dependency-worthiness, code quality, security, resource management, performance, and operations.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Payloads have staged, finalized, failed, and orphaned lifecycle states.
- Large inputs are streamed with endpoint-specific limits.
- Reconciliation detects and safely handles missing or orphaned data.
- Backup and restore pair database and object-store evidence.
- Partial failures are covered by repeatable tests.

### [x] EVY-401: Implement a staged payload lifecycle

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `XL`
- Depends on: `EVY-203, EVY-201`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `migrations/`
- `internal/app/object_ingestion.go (new)`
- `internal/app/ports.go`
- `internal/adapters/objectstore/filesystem/`
- `internal/adapters/objectstore/s3/`
- `internal/adapters/postgres/repositories/`

**Implementation:**
1. Create payload metadata states `staged`, `finalized`, `failed`, and `orphaned` with tenant, digest, size, media type, staging key, final key, and timestamps.
2. Stream to a tenant-scoped staging key while hashing and counting bytes.
3. Commit the domain record and staged metadata transaction, then finalize or copy the object using an idempotent worker operation.
4. Treat a domain record as usable only when its payload is finalized and digest verified.
5. Make repeated finalization safe after a crash.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Database failure after upload leaves a discoverable staged object, not hidden state.
- Object finalization failure leaves a retryable record.
- Readers never trust a non-finalized payload.
- The same digest can be safely deduplicated within a tenant.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/objectstore/... ./internal/adapters/postgres`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
feat(storage): add staged payload finalization (EVY-401)
```

**Completion evidence:**
- `efbda5d` (`feat(storage): add staged payload finalization (EVY-401)`) adds tenant-scoped staged/finalized/failed/orphaned payload metadata, streaming filesystem and S3-compatible staging, digest verification, idempotent finalization, transactional domain/outbox linkage, and reader/worker lifecycle enforcement.
- 2026-07-28: `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/objectstore/... ./internal/adapters/postgres -count=1` passed, including PostgreSQL lifecycle persistence, filesystem behavior, and an S3 protocol integration test.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-60: Cryptographic Verification And Trust-Policy Enforcement

**Objective:** Replace metadata capture with real cryptographic verification, explicit trust policies, historical key semantics, and independently reviewable test vectors.

**Score impact:** Dependency-worthiness, security trust, API design, feature completeness, open-source trust, and ecosystem fit.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Sigstore/Cosign and DSSE verification are cryptographic and policy aware.
- Signing providers sign canonical payload hashes through explicit profiles.
- Historical verification respects key rotation and revocation.
- Transparency proof behavior is exact and bounded.
- Release and package verification fail closed on ambiguity.

### [x] EVY-601: Write the cryptographic trust-model ADR and verification profiles

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `L`
- Depends on: `EVY-102, EVY-501`

**Implementation:**
1. Define what is signed for release bundles, customer package manifests, artifact signatures, DSSE attestations, Merkle checkpoints, and public release manifests.
2. Define canonicalization, hash algorithm, signature algorithm, trusted root, identity policy, issuer policy, transparency requirement, clock policy, and revocation semantics for each profile.
3. Separate `recorded metadata`, `cryptographically valid`, `identity trusted`, and `transparency verified`.
4. Define offline verification inputs and how missing network evidence affects results.
5. Specify algorithm agility and version migration.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Every verification endpoint maps to a named profile.
- A profile states all mandatory and optional checks.
- No profile uses vague terms such as secure or trusted without criteria.
- The ADR includes threat boundaries and non-goals.

**Required validation:**
- `make docs-check`
- `make quality-scorecard-check`


registry of named verification profiles. The registry fixes canonical inputs,
hash/signature algorithms, trust-root, identity, issuer, transparency, clock,
revocation, offline, required-check, and optional-check semantics. Implemented
verification paths now use stable profile constants; the verification reference
maps every current HTTP verification route and offline package/release verifier
to a named profile while explicitly recording paths that are still limited or
await profile-bearing receipts in EVY-606. The ADR separates recorded metadata,
cryptographic validity, identity policy, and transparency proof treatment, and
documents threat boundaries, non-goals, algorithm agility, and append-only
profile migration. Compatibility: existing profile IDs and receipt schemas are
preserved; no historical receipt was rewritten. Validated with

`make quality-scorecard-check`, `make finalize`, and `make lint`.

**Implementation:**

2. Support key-based and keyless bundle verification for the profiles declared in EVY-601.
3. Verify signature bytes, subject digest, certificate chain, Fulcio root, certificate validity, expected identity, expected issuer, signed bundle material, and Rekor proof or explicit offline policy.
4. Require caller or tenant policy to supply expected identity and issuer. Do not trust values copied from the signature record itself.
5. Persist a safe verification receipt with library version, profile, trust-root version, checks, and limitations.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Valid official Sigstore vectors pass.
- Invalid signature, wrong digest, wrong identity, wrong issuer, expired certificate, and bad proof fail.
- Network unavailability cannot silently downgrade a required online profile.
- The old metadata-assessment route is removed or clearly deprecated.


operator-supplied versioned trust material and explicit offline-only profiles.
The adapter verifies official keyless bundles; signature, digest, identity,
issuer, certificate-at-observer-time, trust-root, and Rekor-proof failures are
covered by regression tests. Key verification accepts configured public-key
trust material and keeps Rekor inclusion mandatory. The HTTP contract rejects
legacy metadata-assessment fields and requires an explicit key/keyless offline
policy; online-required requests fail closed without a network downgrade.

Validated successfully with `go test ./internal/adapters/verification/sigstore
./internal/app`, `go test -race ./...`, `make release-check`, and Docker-backed
`make production-check` (live PostgreSQL, migration compatibility, 80.0%

the former metadata-only Cosign request fields are rejected; callers must supply
the keyless expected identity and issuer, and the current implementation
deliberately supports explicit offline bundles only.

### [x] EVY-605: Make key rotation, revocation, and historical validity explicit

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `L`
- Depends on: `EVY-604, EVY-104`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/domain/signing/`

- `docs/runbooks/key-rotation.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Store key version, provider, public material or fingerprint, valid-from, valid-until, revoked-at, revocation reason, and compromise semantics.
2. Distinguish ordinary retirement from compromise. A compromised key can invalidate historical results according to profile policy.
3. Use the injected clock for identifiers and validity checks; remove direct `time.Now()` dependencies from domain logic.
4. Prevent two active keys for a profile when the profile requires one.
5. Add rotation and emergency revocation rehearsals.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Historical verification is deterministic for a specified verification time.
- Retired and compromised keys have different behavior.
- Concurrent rotations cannot create an invalid active-key set.
- Runbooks include rollback and package re-verification guidance.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/postgres`
- `make migration-compatibility-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
feat(signing): version key lifecycle and revocation (EVY-605)
```

**Completion evidence:** Implemented in `2082f03cfcd7b37fa97708b3cd08d95e08d3a499`. Tenant signing keys now record version, provider, public-key fingerprint, validity interval, revocation reason/semantics, historical-validity policy, and compromise time. Rotation derives key identifiers from the injected clock, retires prior keys with a validity end, and PostgreSQL plus the in-memory unit of work enforce one active tenant/provider key. Historical verification uses persisted signature/key timestamps and explicit policy: ordinary revocation preserves valid historical signatures, while compromise can invalidate from compromise time or all history. Migration compatibility preserves legacy public material and revoked-key upper bounds. OpenAPI and the key-rotation runbook now expose the lifecycle policy, rotation/emergency-revocation rehearsals, forward-only rollback, and customer-package re-verification. `go test ./internal/app ./internal/adapters/postgres` coverage was included in the final live-PostgreSQL production gate; `PATH=/tmp/evy602-postgres16-bin:$PATH EVYDENCE_TEST_DATABASE_URL='postgres://evydence:change-me@localhost:5432/evydence?sslmode=disable' make migration-compatibility-check` passed in 90.493s; `PATH=/tmp/evy602-postgres16-bin:$PATH EVYDENCE_TEST_DATABASE_URL='postgres://evydence:change-me@localhost:5432/evydence?sslmode=disable' make production-check` passed on 2026-08-15, including migration application, race tests, 80.0% coverage, lint, gosec, govulncheck, release acceptance, benchmark, API/worker/PostgreSQL restart, release-artifact black-box, and release-signing smoke checks. Compatibility: legacy records retain their persisted public material; migration derives lifecycle fields from existing facts, so legacy records may have an empty fingerprint and revocation reason until a new lifecycle event is recorded.

`subject_ref` request fields are removed from OpenAPI, the Go and TypeScript
SDKs, and checked examples. Artifact `media_type` is required and is rejected
before either in-memory or PostgreSQL-backed persistence. The release and
artifact response schemas/examples now match the domain JSON fields. The
remaining typed build helper was also corrected during the required helper
sweep: `CreateBuildRequest` now exposes the handler's CI identity,
`finished_at`, and `provider_metadata` fields; obsolete `completed_at` and
`github` fields and the unsupported nested build-output name are removed.
`scripts/sdk_check.py` now compares OpenAPI properties and requiredness against
Go SDK types, TypeScript types, and handler decode structs for release,
artifact, and build requests; its regression tests are in
`scripts/test_sdk_check.py`, and the checker is part of `make sdk-check`.
`CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/runbooks/upgrade.md` give prerelease client migration
guidance. `go test ./internal/adapters/httpapi ./sdk/go/evydence` passed;
`python3 scripts/test_sdk_check.py` passed; `make openapi-check` passed;

EVYDENCE_TEST_DATABASE_URL='postgres://evydence:change-me@localhost:5432/evydence?sslmode=disable'
make production-check` passed with exit code 0 on 2026-08-15, including lint,

### [x] EVY-703: Add bounded pagination, deterministic sorting, and conditional reads

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P1`
- Size: `XL`
- Depends on: `EVY-701, EVY-304`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/adapters/httpapi/`
- `internal/app/query/ (new or refactored)`
- `internal/adapters/postgres/repositories/`
- `openapi.yaml`
- `docs/api.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Replace unbounded list operations with cursor-based pagination using stable sort keys and opaque cursors.
2. Define default and maximum page sizes, allowed sort fields, filter validation, and next-page metadata.
3. Add ETags for immutable resources and version-based ETags for mutable administrative resources.
4. Use indexed database queries rather than loading full tenant maps.
5. Keep backwards compatibility through transitional defaults or versioned endpoints.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Large tenants cannot cause unbounded response or memory growth.
- Pagination has no duplicates or omissions across stable data.
- Invalid cursors fail safely.
- OpenAPI and SDKs expose typed page envelopes.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/adapters/httpapi ./internal/adapters/postgres`
- `make openapi-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
feat(api): add cursor pagination and etags (EVY-703)
```

**Completion evidence:**
- Implementation: `8999ea0aca64c33021dcebb227fdeea20e8d9c72` — `feat(api): add cursor pagination and etags (EVY-703)`.

The temporary wrappers use the PostgreSQL 16 client tools from the Docker service, matching the Docker PostgreSQL 16 server; the host PostgreSQL 18 client cannot restore its dump to that server.

Conditional reads apply only to finite JSON resource reads and return private ETags with `Vary: Authorization`.

## [x] EPIC EVY-90: Bounded Contexts And Maintainable Architecture

**Objective:** Replace the process-wide Ledger god aggregate with cohesive services, focused domain packages, query models, and an explicit composition root.

**Score impact:** Code quality, architecture, testability, performance, API stability, and maintainer burden.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Production command handling no longer depends on one giant mutable aggregate.
- Domain ownership and dependency direction are explicit.
- Services are independently testable through focused ports.
- Queries do not scan process-wide maps.
- Architecture checks fail on boundary violations.

**Epic completion evidence (2026-10-10):** EVY-901 through EVY-906 are complete.
Context-owned models/ports and native PostgreSQL command/query composition replace
the process-wide mutable Ledger; production queries do not use its maps.
The passing EVY-906 full unit/race/production gates and real forbidden-import
regression cover the exit criteria. See its completion record for exact commands,
source commits, remaining size/interface review flags and local-evidence limits.
This does not raise the independent quality score or claim hosted CI,
publication, provider controls or multi-writer safety.

- `internal/verification/app/ (new)`
- `internal/app/vex.go`
- `internal/app/governance_packages.go`
- `internal/app/integrity_runtime.go`

**Implementation:**
1. Extract vulnerability decision lifecycle, VEX mapping, exceptions, approvals, readiness policy, package generation, redaction, signing, and verification orchestration.
2. Keep report generation read-only except for explicitly persisted report records and receipts.
3. Separate cryptographic adapters from policy aggregation.
4. Ensure package generation uses a committed snapshot or transaction-consistent read view.
5. Remove broad cross-resource map access.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Decision and package services have explicit inputs and repositories.
- A package cannot observe a half-committed release state.
- Verification policy is testable without network providers.
- The old god aggregate no longer owns these workflows.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./...`
- `make demo-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
refactor(app): extract decision package and verification services (EVY-904)
```

**Completion evidence:**

- Commit: `0af5f40 refactor(app): extract decision package and verification services (EVY-904)`.
- Added focused risk, package, and verification application services with explicit tenant-scoped readers, transactional repositories, authorization, clock/ID, audit/outbox, signing, canonicalization, and provider ports. Ledger and HTTP adapters preserve existing route and call shapes while the focused services own decision/VEX, exception/approval/readiness, package/redaction/report, and signing/verification workflows. Policy and report assembly use committed snapshots; only explicitly persisted records and receipts write state.
- Transaction-fake and compatibility tests cover tenant isolation, read-only reports, committed package snapshots, rollback on persistence/audit failure, conservative provider failure, signing-key lifecycle, trust-policy validation, and malformed bundle imports. An imported evidence-bundle manifest with repeated evidence IDs is now rejected instead of silently collapsing duplicates. The transitive gRPC dependency was updated to `v1.83.1` to resolve the called-vulnerability gate without changing the public API.
- Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.13 go test ./...`; `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.13 make demo-check`; and `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.13 make production-check` with `.test.env.example` loaded, live PostgreSQL and MinIO, and PostgreSQL 16 client tools. The production gate passed `make finalize`, lint (`0` issues), gosec (`0` issues), govulncheck (`0` called vulnerabilities), race tests, live PostgreSQL/S3 checks, migration compatibility, the regression benchmark, black-box API/worker/restart/package/audit-chain and release-artifact checks, and release-signing verification. Coverage met both floors: `80.0%` total versus `80.0%` required and `81.2%` critical-package versus `81.0%` required.
- Compatibility: existing API route shapes and Ledger facades remain for callers while EVY-905 completes composition/query migration. Previously accepted malformed imports containing duplicate manifest evidence IDs now fail validation; valid signed bundles remain compatible. Production-signing and third-party provider claims remain limited to the local gate evidence recorded above.

- Priority: `P1`

**Implementation:**
1. Build all services and adapters in one composition root selected by explicit runtime profile.
2. Make handler dependencies interfaces or concrete focused services, not a service locator.
3. Implement database-backed queries with pagination and transaction-consistent projections.
4. Keep optional caches bounded, observable, invalidated after commit, and never authoritative.
5. Make local memory mode an explicit profile with visible limitations.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- API and worker entry points share validated wiring rules.
- Handlers cannot reach unrelated context internals.
- Production reads do not require loading all tenant state.
- Cache loss or stale cache cannot corrupt durable behavior.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./...`
- `make black-box-demo-check`
- `make production-check`

```text
refactor(runtime): add focused composition and query services (EVY-905)
```

All independently committed implementation units are listed below.
- Removed the production Ledger type, configuration, constructor, maps, global mutex/transaction gate, cached authorization and snapshot/replay forwarding. These cover the ticket's complete unit/race command surfaces, including native HTTP/worker wiring and unchanged historical behavior assertions.

Its unchanged recipes ran `make integration-check`, `make release-check` (including finalize, release acceptance, security regressions, full lint/gosec/govulncheck, race, live migrations and PostgreSQL integration), `make coverage-check`, `make migration-compatibility-check`, `make benchmark-check`, `make black-box-demo-check`, `make black-box-release-artifact-check`, and release manifest/signature verification.

Full govulncheck: 0 called vulnerabilities; 2 imported-package and 5 required-module advisories without called symbols remain recorded, not suppressed.

The local regression benchmark passed (1909785 ns/op, 634079 B/op, 3488 allocs/op; checked black-box scenario 6065.922 ms), not a production-capacity claim.

A new saturated-single-connection regression failed before the fix; independent read-only package-preparation capacity resolved it. The original 16-caller replay/count test and its 45-second deadline stayed unchanged and passed both focused checks and full gates. Wrong-tenant reads/signing, committed-only snapshots, valid signatures, cancellation and connection release are also covered.

Benchmark and binary summaries identify tested source `3c2f1037c0a9`; these are local artifacts, not published release assets.
- Compatibility: API/OpenAPI, persistence, immutable canonical/hash, signature and replay formats are unchanged. The formerly supported `local_memory` API profile is retired as explicitly approved; local evaluation requires PostgreSQL. Each Store may open up to `min(primary pool_max_conns, 4)` additional read-only preparation connections, documented in the capacity reference.
- Environment/limits: disposable digest-pinned PostgreSQL 16.14 on bounded tmpfs with fsync, synchronous_commit and full_page_writes enabled; matching PostgreSQL 16 recovery clients; the exact digest-pinned locally cached MinIO image; configured committed test placeholders. The production recipe serialized package binaries through `GOFLAGS=-p=1` without changing test concurrency, assertions, fixture deadlines, race instrumentation or coverage floors. This proves the checked logical/restart/backup-restore profile, not host-reboot/power-loss durability, hosted CI, cold registry pulls, external-provider controls, multi-writer safety, release publication or legal/security sufficiency.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-100: Security Hardening And Abuse Resistance

**Objective:** Model realistic threats and harden authorization, HTTP behavior, outbound requests, sessions, secrets, parsers, archives, and resource usage.

**Score impact:** Security, dependency-worthiness, operations, open-source trust, API quality, and test trust.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- A current threat model covers all trust boundaries.
- Authorization is centralized and exhaustively tested.
- Inbound and outbound HTTP behavior is bounded and SSRF resistant.
- Session and secret handling follow explicit security properties.
- Resource-exhaustion and leakage regressions are tested.

### [x] EVY-1001: Create a current threat model and security requirements matrix

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `L`
- Depends on: `EVY-901, EVY-601`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `docs/security/threat-model.md (new)`
- `docs/security/security-requirements.md (new)`
- `SECURITY.md`
- `docs/reference/source-of-truth.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Model API clients, collectors, customer portal users, administrators, PostgreSQL, object storage, workers, KMS/HSM, identity providers, transparency providers, CI, release workflows, and malicious evidence inputs.
2. Cover tenant escape, confused deputy, SSRF, replay, signature substitution, parser bombs, archive traversal, credential theft, database rewrite, object mismatch, and supply-chain compromise.
3. Map each threat to a requirement, implementation control, test, operator control, residual risk, and owner.
4. Mark unsupported threat profiles explicitly.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Every public trust claim has a mapped control and test.
- External operator controls are not represented as repo-owned.
- High-risk unresolved threats are P0 or P1 backlog items.
- The model is versioned by commit and reviewed before releases.

**Required validation:**
- `make docs-check`
- `make quality-scorecard-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
docs(security): add threat model and controls matrix (EVY-1001)
```

**Completion evidence:**

- Commit: `c09e422 docs(security): add threat model and controls matrix (EVY-1001)`.
- Added `docs/security/threat-model.md` and `docs/security/security-requirements.md`; updated `SECURITY.md` and the documentation source map. The model covers API clients, collectors, portal users, administrators, PostgreSQL, object storage, workers, signing/KMS/HSM, identity/transparency providers, CI/release workflows, and malicious evidence input. The matrix maps tenant escape/confused deputy, SSRF, replay, signature substitution, parser/archive abuse, credential theft, database rewrite, object mismatch, supply-chain compromise, leakage, diagnostics, idempotency, and worker failure to requirements, concrete repository evidence/tests, external controls, residual risk, and an owner.
- The model labels centralized authorization (EVY-1002), outbound SSRF protection (EVY-1004), SSO/session hardening (EVY-1005), and complete secret/resource controls (EVY-1006) as unresolved P0 work; it also records the linked P0/P1 test, logging, configuration, and deployment work. Privileged infrastructure access, provider truth, and legal sufficiency remain explicitly external rather than fabricated repository completion claims.
- Passed: requirement-ID and cited-local-test-path cross-check; `make docs-check`; `make quality-scorecard-check`; and `make finalize` (unit/API, precise OpenAPI, docs, SDK, reviewer package, and related project-owned checks).
- Compatibility: documentation only. No API, runtime, persistence, provider, or deployment contract changed. The release-review rule is a repository policy requirement and does not claim that maintainers, operators, or external reviewers have performed a review.

### [x] EVY-1002: Centralize authorization policy and prove route coverage

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `XL`
- Depends on: `EVY-901, EVY-903`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/authz/ (new)`
- `internal/adapters/httpapi/route_registration.go`
- `internal/*/app/`
- `internal/app/authz_inventory_test.go`
- `openapi.yaml`

**Implementation:**
1. Define typed actions and resource references rather than scattered scope strings and hidden checks.
2. Evaluate tenant, organization, product, project, release, environment, package, and instance constraints in one policy layer.
3. Generate route-to-action coverage and fail if a route lacks policy, scope, tenant behavior, and negative tests.
4. Keep cross-tenant reads indistinguishable where required, while preserving operator diagnostics behind instance admin.
5. Add collector and SSO actor-specific tests.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Every route has positive and negative authorization tests.
- Wildcard tenant admin does not imply instance admin.
- Collectors cannot claim identity or resources outside their binding.
- No application service bypasses the policy layer.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/authz ./internal/adapters/httpapi ./internal/...`
- `make openapi-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
refactor(authz): centralize tenant and resource policy (EVY-1002)
```

**Completion evidence (2026-10-11):**

- Validated clean source: `50f4ad371bc4bce992e1b3cad689c0eeeb7876cf`.
  Final source/test correction: `8fbb2c1e`; complete route behavior matrix:
  `2fa7ef2f`; route-policy inventory: `240f16e7`. Central policy began in
  `fb8a3232`; collector binding/attribution are `61ea5953`/`4c7617ad`, portal
  policy `78db178a`, signed receivers `73ddfccf`, SSO exchange `70c11bdf`.
  Context/action migrations and source guards are in the intervening EVY-1002
  commits. This completion tracking changes documentation only.
- `internal/authz` owns typed actions, closed resource-reference shapes,
  current grants, collector bindings, explicit instance administration,
  self-session ownership and verified public-credential boundaries. Focused
  command/query delegates select their own actions; shared capabilities do not
  permit action substitution or legacy scope-request fallback. Source guards
  reject local application/query grant decisions. Tenant/organization
  administration stays own-tenant; narrower policy uses resolved current
  ownership rather than caller-supplied tenant or parent claims.
- All 189 API operations declare authentication mode, applicable actions/scopes
  and tenant behavior. Registration rejects missing, unknown or drifting contracts.
  `TestRouteAuthorizationBehaviorMatrix` executes native policy scenarios;
  `TestRouteAuthorizationBehaviorCoverage` rejects missing/unregistered cases.
  Allowed results/effects, missing/insufficient authority, foreign ownership
  and denied-effect invariants are asserted. Actor-specific cases prove tenant
  wildcard/grants cannot imply instance power, collectors cannot claim another

### [x] EVY-1003: Harden inbound HTTP, trusted proxies, and rate limiting

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `L`
- Depends on: `EVY-704, EVY-402`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `cmd/evydence-api/main.go`
- `internal/adapters/httpapi/router.go`
- `internal/adapters/httpapi/middleware/ (new)`
- `docs/reference/configuration.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Set read-header, read, write, idle, and graceful-shutdown timeouts from validated configuration.
2. Limit headers, URLs, multipart parts if used, decompressed bytes, concurrent uploads, and total in-flight requests.
3. Replace the unbounded in-memory IP bucket map with a bounded limiter or external-store option.
4. Trust forwarded client addresses only from configured proxy CIDRs. Otherwise use the direct remote address.
5. Define per-route and per-tenant limits for expensive operations and return safe retry metadata.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- A reverse proxy cannot spoof arbitrary client identities unless trusted.
- Rate-limit state is bounded and expires.
- Slowloris and oversized-header tests fail safely.
- Expensive uploads cannot starve all normal requests.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./cmd/evydence-api ./internal/adapters/httpapi`
- `go test -race ./...`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
fix(http): bound requests proxies and rate limits (EVY-1003)
```

**Completion evidence:**

- Commits: `72f83fe fix(http): bound requests proxies and rate limits (EVY-1003)` and `036e4ca test(http): prove rate-limit expiry (EVY-1003)`.
- Added validated read-header, read, write, idle, and graceful-shutdown listener configuration; bounded request-target, body, in-flight, and native-upload processing; and a cancellable graceful server shutdown.
- Added a capped, expiring limiter. It accepts `X-Forwarded-For` only when the direct peer matches `EVYDENCE_TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS`, otherwise it uses the direct TCP address. Expensive document, bundle, package/report, diff, and policy routes are limited independently per authenticated tenant and route.
- Passed: `go test ./cmd/evydence-api ./internal/adapters/httpapi -count=1`, including proxy-spoofing, bucket-capacity/expiry, content-encoding/multipart, concurrency, tenant/route, slow-header, and oversized-header regression tests; the expiry follow-up also passed its focused test and `make test-race`.
- Passed: `make test-race`, `make lint`, `make openapi-check`, `make docs-check`, `make deploy-check`, and `make finalize`.
- Passed: `set -a; . ./.test.env.example; set +a; PATH=/tmp/evy703-pg16.qSXpPa:$PATH make production-check` against the live Docker PostgreSQL 16 service: release checks, gosec, govulncheck, race, migrations, PostgreSQL integration, 80.1% coverage, benchmark, API/worker black-box flows, and release-style signing smoke test all passed.
- Compatibility: compressed and multipart request bodies are now rejected because no public contract supports them. Existing retryable RFC 9457 `429` responses remain the overload response; documented process limits can be set to `0` only for controlled local evaluation.

### [x] EVY-1004: Make outbound HTTP clients SSRF resistant and policy controlled

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `L`
- Depends on: `EVY-1001`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/adapters/identity/oidcdiscovery/`
- `internal/adapters/identity/oidcuserinfo/`
- `internal/adapters/identity/httpvalidator/`
- `internal/adapters/transparency/httpfetcher/`
- `internal/adapters/transparency/httpgateway/`
- `internal/adapters/signing/httpgateway/`
- `internal/platform/httpclient/ (new)`

**Implementation:**
1. Create one hardened outbound client factory with finite timeouts, response limits, TLS minimums, redirect policy, DNS/IP validation, and proxy policy.
2. Reject loopback, link-local, multicast, metadata-service, private, and newly resolved prohibited addresses unless an explicit local-development allowlist applies.
3. Revalidate each redirect and defend against DNS rebinding.
4. Never forward bearer tokens to a redirected or different origin.
5. Add destination allowlists for gateway and provider profiles.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Metadata-service and private-network SSRF vectors fail.
- Redirect and DNS rebinding tests fail closed.
- Localhost exceptions are impossible in production mode.
- Sensitive headers are origin scoped.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/adapters/identity/... ./internal/adapters/transparency/... ./internal/adapters/signing/...`
- `gosec ./...`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
fix(security): enforce outbound destination policy (EVY-1004)
```

**Completion evidence:**

- Commit: `4b3ae6b8ba4eec64b2c3101c10ecfc5354ff3561 fix(security): enforce outbound destination policy (EVY-1004)`.
- Added `internal/platform/httpclient`, a shared outbound policy used by OIDC discovery/UserInfo, provider-validation, signing-gateway, transparency-gateway, and transparency-fetch adapters. It enforces finite timeouts and response budgets, TLS 1.2+, no ambient proxy, profile host allowlists, redirect denial, resolution before every dial with direct checked-IP dialing, and rejection of loopback, private, link-local/metadata, multicast, unspecified, carrier-grade NAT, and reserved/documentation IPv4 addresses. OIDC discovery and UserInfo are same-origin, so a supplied bearer token cannot cross to a discovered different origin.
- Production startup now rejects all six `EVYDENCE_*_ALLOW_INSECURE_LOCALHOST=true` outbound-provider overrides. The narrowly scoped loopback HTTP exception remains available only outside production; configuration documentation records the changed contract and the deliberate lack of ambient proxy support.
- Added regression coverage for private/metadata targets, DNS rebinding to loopback, redirect/header non-forwarding, profile-host allowlisting, response limits, cross-origin OIDC UserInfo rejection, and production localhost-override rejection. Tests use local fakes only; no provider credentials, bearer tokens, or real provider calls are used.
- Passed: `go test ./internal/platform/httpclient ./internal/adapters/identity/... ./internal/adapters/transparency/... ./internal/adapters/signing/... ./cmd/evydence-api`; `gosec ./...` (0 issues); `make lint` (0 issues); `make docs-check`; and `set -a; . ./.test.env.example; set +a; PATH=/tmp/evy703-pg16.qSXpPa:$PATH make production-check` against Docker PostgreSQL 16 and MinIO. The final gate passed finalize, release acceptance, lint, gosec, govulncheck (no reachable vulnerabilities), race, migrations, live PostgreSQL/object-store integration, 80.2% coverage, benchmark, API/worker restart/demo, release artifact, and signing smoke checks.
- Compatibility: gateway/provider adapters now fail closed for redirects, private destinations, cross-origin OIDC UserInfo, and ambient proxy use. Production configurations that previously enabled an insecure localhost exception now fail startup; non-production loopback tests remain supported. Private-provider or corporate-proxy deployments require a separately reviewed deployment/code change with explicit egress and IAM controls.

- Revoked/expired cookie credentials clear safely; an invalid explicit bearer does not use or clear an incidental cookie.
- SSO credential responses cannot be stored by HTTP caches.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-110: High-Trust Test System And Compatibility Proof

**Objective:** Turn the broad but uneven test suite into evidence for failure safety, concurrency, standards compatibility, upgrades, release artifacts, and supported platforms.

**Score impact:** Test quality, dependency-worthiness, architecture, maintenance trust, and release stability.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Critical behavior has explicit positive, negative, boundary, and failure tests.
- Live PostgreSQL and object-store tests are mandatory for production gates.
- Fuzz, race, stress, and fault-injection tests cover high-risk paths.
- Upgrades from published releases are tested.
- Black-box binaries and images pass the real user workflow.

### [x] EVY-1102: Create systematic persistence and crash atomicity tests

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `XL`

**Implementation:**
1. Inject failures before insert, after domain insert, after audit insert, after outbox insert, before commit, after commit, and before response write.
2. Assert failed pre-commit commands are invisible and committed post-response-loss commands are replayable.
3. Cover credentials, evidence, decisions, bundles, packages, signatures, verification results, and worker side effects.
4. Restart API and worker between kill points.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Every authoritative write family has atomicity coverage.
- No failure leaves a partial externally visible record set.
- Post-commit transport failure is resolved by idempotent replay.
- Fault hooks are test-only and cannot be enabled in production accidentally.

**Required validation:**
- `scripts/fault_injection_check.sh`
- `go test ./internal/adapters/postgres ./internal/...`
- `make production-check`


## [ ] EPIC EVY-120: Operational Readiness, Observability, And Deployment Safety

**Objective:** Make the service operable through graceful lifecycle management, structured diagnostics, meaningful health checks, metrics, tracing, validated configuration, worker controls, and hardened deployment artifacts.

**Score impact:** Dependency-worthiness, operations, documentation, security, performance, and production stability.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- API and workers terminate gracefully and report exact build identity.
- Operators have actionable logs, metrics, traces, readiness, and alerts.
- Configuration is validated from one schema.
- Migrations and worker failure handling are safe.
- Reference deployments and runbooks match tested behavior.

### [x] EVY-1201: Add graceful shutdown and complete server lifecycle controls

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `L`
- Depends on: `EVY-105, EVY-1003, EVY-1207, EVY-1208, EVY-1209`

**Execution decomposition (2026-10-15):** Preserve every original requirement
and acceptance criterion below. Implement worker drain/lease safety (EVY-1207),
HTTP drain/forced failure (EVY-1208), then resource ownership and deployment
termination budgets (EVY-1209). Each unit needs its focused checks and local
final gate; the parent stays incomplete until all units and the original full
production/release-binary checks pass. EVY-1105's independent hosted-publication
and required-check acceptance is tracked in its own entry.

**Initial target files or components:**
- `cmd/evydence-api/main.go`
- `cmd/evydence-worker/main.go`
- `internal/platform/runtime/ (new)`
- `docs/operations.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Use signal-aware root contexts for API and worker processes.
2. Stop accepting new API work, allow bounded in-flight completion, then close database, object, provider, and telemetry resources in dependency order.
3. Stop workers from claiming new jobs, release or safely expire leases, and finish or cancel current jobs according to job semantics.
4. Treat `http.ErrServerClosed` as normal shutdown.
5. Expose shutdown timeout and drain status.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- SIGTERM during a normal request does not corrupt state.
- SIGTERM during a worker job leaves it safely retryable or completed.
- Shutdown exits with success when graceful and nonzero on forced failure.
- Kubernetes termination grace settings match the application timeout.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./cmd/evydence-api ./cmd/evydence-worker`
- `make black-box-release-artifact-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
feat(runtime): add graceful api and worker shutdown (EVY-1201)
```

**Completion evidence (2026-10-16):** All three units are complete: worker drain
`1753cdee05a219aa45f5f3aebeb82ca073540bce`, HTTP drain
`e5e9b51e6c90ddb55a591ceb45ef07b75ab35bda`, and final resource/deployment/binary
implementation `074648d883c786c2a265a280a04e856fafd96ef4`. The original API/worker
tests, `make black-box-release-artifact-check` and `make production-check` all
passed on the clean final implementation; detailed commands, archive hashes and
limits are recorded under EVY-1209. Four real SIGTERM cases prove graceful exit
`0`, forced exit `1`, durable request replay and fenced native worker recovery.
Helm/Compose rendering validates matching termination budgets. This is local
release-style binary evidence, without a live cluster, published release assets
or external-provider interoperability claim. Uncertain commits still require
idempotent retry; worker execution is not promised exactly once.

### [x] EVY-1207: Drain workers with bounded completion and retryable leases

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `M`
- Depends on: `EVY-105, EVY-1003, EVY-305`
- Parent: `EVY-1201`

**Scope:** Keep signal-aware admission separate from the current job lifetime.
Stop new claims and unstarted batch work on shutdown, allow bounded in-flight
completion, and cancel on the deadline without acknowledging uncertain work.
Preserve lease fencing, idempotent effects and safe expiry/reclaim. Validate the
worker shutdown budget and report safe lifecycle status; normal shutdown exits
successfully and forced failure exits nonzero.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Idle/poll/claim cancellation stops normally without another claim.
- A job finishing during drain is acknowledged once; no later batch job starts.
- Deadline cancellation leaves unfinished work reclaimable without duplicate
  effects or a false completion/failure acknowledgement.
- Signal, cancellation, failure and safe diagnostics have deterministic tests;
  real PostgreSQL evidence covers lease retry/completion semantics.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./cmd/evydence-worker -count=1`
- `go test -race ./cmd/evydence-worker -count=1`
- `make finalize`
- Original EVY-1201 full production/release-binary acceptance before parent closure.

**Completion evidence (2026-10-15):** Implementation
`1753cdee05a219aa45f5f3aebeb82ca073540bce` on `master`:

- Signal cancellation stops claims and batch admission, succeeds after graceful
  drain, and gives processing plus acknowledgement one bounded drain budget.
  The daemon validates `EVYDENCE_WORKER_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` before opening
  storage (default 30; whole seconds 1–300). Deadline expiry cancels work, exits
  nonzero and avoids blocking pool cleanup. Payload-free lifecycle logs expose
  start/completion; raw provider/database errors are not newly logged.
- Original five shutdown regressions failed before the fix; cleanup and typed-nil
  negative tests also failed first. Worker tests include actual SIGTERM helper
  processes, deadline/uncertain acknowledgements and preserved failure classes.
- `go test ./cmd/evydence-worker -count=1 -timeout=5m` PASS (5.414s);
  `go test -race ./cmd/evydence-worker -count=1 -timeout=5m` PASS (8.228s), both
  with PostgreSQL 16.14. Native payload tests cover graceful completion,
  cancellation before effects and commit before timeout. Reclaim changes tokens,
  rejects stale completion, preserves tenant scope and retries idempotently;
  the aggregate-loading canary remains active. Lease duration/schema unchanged.
- Clean implementation commit: `make finalize`, `make lint`, `make gosec`,
  `make vuln` all PASS through sequential foreground `codex-ramgate 8`;
  scoped checks/restoration/archiving use `codex-ramgate 4`. Lint/gosec: 0 issues.
  Govulncheck: 0 called vulnerabilities; 2 imported-package/5 required-module
  uncalled advisory notices retained. The initially stopped live database caused
  a recorded connection failure; restoration applied 69 migrations before the
  successful full worker checks. No required unit gate was skipped.
- Private VM-partition archive `evidence/evy1207-1753cdee`: 29 files with every
  copy hash verified and source bytes matched to the tested commit; manifest
  SHA-256 `5c864f25a445c89c023f1d7bc928d8a7ff82319a40bc78185342d8f0d2923664`.
  Prior red/failure logs remain evidence, not passes.
- Original EVY-1201 production/release-binary acceptance completed under
  EVY-1209 on 2026-10-16, including process/resource/deployment proof. Forced
  calls may live until process exit; in-flight commit outcomes
  can be uncertain, and retryable leases do not promise exactly-once execution.

- `internal/adapters/httpapi/system_handlers.go`
- `deploy/observability/`
- `docs/reference/observability.md`
- `docs/operations.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Add RED metrics for HTTP and provider calls and USE-style metrics for pools, workers, queues, and storage.
2. Expose outbox backlog age, attempts, terminal jobs, parser latency, signing latency, verification failures, object reconciliation, package generation, DB pool, and migration state.
3. Use bounded labels; never use tenant, resource, CVE, URL, or customer values as unbounded labels.
4. Add OpenTelemetry spans with safe attributes and propagation through workers.
5. Update Grafana and Prometheus examples with alert rationale and runbook links.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Operators can diagnose database, object, provider, and worker failure.
- Metric cardinality tests enforce bounded labels.
- Tracing is optional and failure to export does not break requests.
- Alerts link to current runbooks and avoid unsupported SLO claims.

- `go test ./internal/platform/telemetry ./internal/adapters/httpapi`
- `make deploy-check`
- `make production-check`

```text
feat(observability): add bounded metrics and tracing (EVY-1204)
```

### [x] EVY-1210: Define bounded operational observation contracts

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P1`
- Size: `S`
- Depends on: `EVY-1202, EVY-1203, EVY-305`
- Parent: `EVY-1204`

**Scope:** Add the framework-independent observation vocabulary and context port
under `internal/platform/telemetry/`. Operations and outcomes have finite
code-owned values; events contain numeric timing/attempt data and no identities,
URLs, errors, attributes, bodies or arbitrary labels. Unknown values normalize
before delivery. Recording preserves request cancellation and cannot turn an
observer panic into an application failure. Observer implementations must be
nonblocking and own any asynchronous work. This adds no runtime instrumentation
or exporter dependency by itself.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Unsafe operation/outcome canaries never reach a recorder.
- Events are passed by value; normalization cannot mutate the caller's event.
- Context inheritance, explicit disablement, cancellation and nil/panicking
  observers have regression tests.
- The core contract imports no metrics/tracing SDK or transport/storage adapter.

**Required validation:**
- `go test ./internal/platform/telemetry -count=1`
- `go test ./internal/platform/telemetry -race -count=1`
- `make fast-check`

**Completion evidence (2026-10-16):**

- Verified completed dependencies EVY-1202, EVY-1203 and EVY-305 before
  implementation on clean `master` at
  `c2deeb9bb0be0c7f9374cb7369899dc98319779e`. Implementation commit:
  `36869428fe0b644dfd3e327d1a98e8b16db79c81`.
- `internal/platform/telemetry` imports only `context` and `time`. A finite
  operation/outcome vocabulary normalizes arbitrary names before callback
  delivery. Numeric duration/attempt fields have no arbitrary labels, identities,
  errors or bodies. Context scopes preserve cancellation, inherit correctly and
  allow explicit child disablement. Events and catalog results are value copies;
  callback panics and typed-nil observers do not change application outcomes.
- Test-first checks initially failed for the absent contract. Exact commands
  `go test ./internal/platform/telemetry -count=1` and
  `go test ./internal/platform/telemetry -race -count=1` pass on the committed
  source bytes. Tests cover 10,000 unsafe operation/outcome pairs collapsing to
  one safe observation, catalog ownership, caller event immutability, canceled
  contexts, missing/disabled/panicking observers and 32 isolated concurrent
  contexts. Scoped `golangci-lint run ./internal/platform/telemetry/...` and
  `gosec ./internal/platform/telemetry/...` pass with zero issues/suppressions.
  Deliberate nil-context cases use a table-driven negative test after lint
  rejected literal nil arguments; the assertions remain covered.
- Required `make fast-check` passed on clean
  `36869428fe0b644dfd3e327d1a98e8b16db79c81` at
  `2026-10-16T13:01:53Z`, including unit tests, fuzz seeds, strategy/domain/
  architecture checks, OpenAPI precision, docs, deployment, SDK, demos and the
  package viewer. Architecture reports 1088 source files and 19 advisory review
  flags; the unchanged API contract has 194 precise operations.
- Validation used `codex-ramgate` with serial foreground commands, one Go
  CPU/package, `GOMEMLIMIT=1024MiB` and `GOGC=50`; focused and fast gates
  reserved 4 GiB. Archive `evy1210-36869428` contains 15 checksum-verified files,
  source, test-first/fixed logs and clean-head gate metadata. Its manifest
  SHA-256 is
  `a8aa1707b48114ca7d49c9a8067fb852132f9adef1a65c0811d85a58283e80f3`;
  completed EVY-1203 source/tracking manifests were verified unchanged.
- Compatibility and limits: this is an internal observation port, with no
  runtime instrumentation, exporter, endpoint, dependency or persistence change.
  Concrete observers must be concurrent-safe, nonblocking, perform no synchronous
  I/O, retain no caller context and own asynchronous export/shutdown. Runtime
  metrics, operator assets, tracing and the parent EVY-1204 remain incomplete;
  no new hosted CI, real-provider, operational-capacity or release proof is claimed.

### [ ] EVY-1206: Harden migrations, worker operations, Helm, and runbooks

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `XL`
- Depends on: `EVY-1201, EVY-1203, EVY-1204, EVY-1205`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `cmd/evydence-migrate/`
- `internal/adapters/postgres/migrate.go`
- `deploy/helm/evydence/`
- `compose.production-like.yml`
- `docs/runbooks/`
- `docs/reference/hardened-reference-deployment.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Use a dedicated migration job with advisory lock, explicit target version, timeout, checksum validation, and no concurrent API migration race.
2. Add worker drain, dead-letter inspection, replay, and queue backlog commands.
3. Harden Helm defaults for pod security, network policy, read-only filesystem where possible, resource requests/limits, disruption, service account, probes, and secret refs.
4. Test production-like Compose and Helm-rendered manifests against the current binary.
5. Update incident, backup, object recovery, key rotation, upgrade, and capacity runbooks with telemetry and exact commands.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Startup never races migrations.
- Helm cannot deploy without an immutable image reference under the stable profile.
- Runbooks are executable against the reference deployment.
- Worker replay and migration actions are audited and authorization protected.

**Required validation:**
- `make migration-compatibility-check`
- `make deploy-check`
- `make restore-rehearsal-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
feat(operations): harden migrations workers and deployment (EVY-1206)
```

**Completion evidence:** Add commit SHA(s), exact checks, and any approved compatibility or external evidence before changing this ticket to `[x]`.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-130: Performance, Capacity, And Resource Predictability

**Objective:** Define realistic workloads, remove architectural bottlenecks, publish measured capacity evidence, and block regressions in critical paths.

**Score impact:** Dependency-worthiness, code architecture, operations, performance, feature completeness, and adoption trust.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Workload and resource budgets are explicit.
- Database queries and indexes are measured.
- Unrelated tenants do not serialize behind one global lock.
- Large evidence and package paths are streaming and bounded.
- Release-specific benchmark evidence is public and reproducible.

### [ ] EVY-1301: Define supported workload profiles and performance budgets

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P1`
- Size: `M`
- Depends on: `EVY-1606`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `docs/reference/performance-budgets.md (new)`
- `docs/reference/capacity-and-failures.md`
- `scripts/benchmark_scenarios/ (new)`

**Implementation:**
1. Define small pilot, medium internal, and large evaluation profiles using products, releases, SBOM components, findings, decisions, payload sizes, package sizes, API rate, and worker backlog.
2. Set p50, p95, and p99 latency, memory, CPU, DB connection, outbox age, and package-generation budgets for a documented reference environment.
3. State which numbers are regression budgets rather than universal capacity claims.
4. Tie profiles to actual design-partner or synthetic workload evidence.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Every benchmark declares commit, environment, dependency versions, and limitations.
- No generic scale claim is made from a microbenchmark.
- Budgets cover failure and recovery behavior, not only throughput.
- The supported single-writer profile remains explicit.

**Required validation:**
- `make docs-check`
- `make benchmark-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
docs(perf): define workload and resource budgets (EVY-1301)
```
