### [ ] EVY-1303: Remove global contention and prove per-tenant concurrency

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P1`
- Size: `XL`
- Depends on: `EVY-906, EVY-304, EVY-1301`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `internal/*/app/`
- `internal/adapters/postgres/repositories/`
- `scripts/concurrency_benchmark.sh (new)`
- `docs/reference/ha-strategy.md`

**Implementation:**
1. Use database transactions and resource-level concurrency rather than a process-wide mutex.
2. Ensure slow package generation, provider calls, or object reads do not hold locks required by unrelated commands.
3. Benchmark independent tenants, same-tenant independent releases, and contested same-resource writes.
4. Keep the single API replica profile until multi-writer correctness is separately proven; do not turn this ticket into an HA claim.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Unrelated tenant requests execute concurrently.
- Contested writes return deterministic conflict or serialization behavior.
- No external network call occurs inside a long database lock unnecessarily.
- Race and contention benchmarks meet the defined pilot budget.

**Required validation:**
- `scripts/concurrency_benchmark.sh`
- `go test -race ./...`
- `make benchmark-check`
- `make production-check`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
perf(concurrency): remove process-wide request serialization (EVY-1303)
```

**Completion evidence:** Add commit SHA(s), exact checks, and any approved compatibility or external evidence before changing this ticket to `[x]`.

### [ ] EVY-1304: Bound memory and worker throughput for ingestion and package generation

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P1`
- Size: `XL`

- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`

**Implementation:**
1. Stream parser input, package archives, report output, and downloads rather than accumulating complete payload graphs where avoidable.
2. Add bounded worker concurrency by job class and provider rate limits.
3. Measure allocations and peak RSS for large supported documents and packages.
4. Add backpressure when object storage, signing, or report providers are slow.
5. Run benchmark budgets in CI with regression tolerances and publish exact-run artifacts.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- Peak memory is bounded by documented input and concurrency limits.
- One large package cannot block all worker classes.
- Benchmark regressions fail with actionable evidence.
- Published results are tied to the release commit and environment.

**Required validation:**
- `make benchmark-check`
- `make production-check`
- `go test ./internal/... -bench . -benchmem`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
perf(runtime): bound ingestion and package resources (EVY-1304)
```

**Completion evidence:** Add commit SHA(s), exact checks, and any approved compatibility or external evidence before changing this ticket to `[x]`.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-140: Supply Chain, Repository Controls, And Release Integrity

**Objective:** Make source, dependencies, builds, containers, release signing, repository settings, changelogs, and revocation independently verifiable.

**Score impact:** Open-source trust, dependency-worthiness, security, release stability, maintenance maturity, and adoption.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Toolchain support is consistent and tested.
- Builds are reproducible and have complete SBOM/provenance.
- Release signing uses protected identity and least privilege.
- Repository controls are verified, not assumed.
- Changelog, release notes, images, and revocation are current.

**Implementation:**

2. Group safe updates, keep security updates prompt, and avoid automatic merge without tests.

4. Document dependency selection and removal criteria.
5. Generate a dependency inventory and stale/unmaintained dependency report.

- New disallowed licenses or severe known vulnerabilities fail CI.
- Exceptions have owner, reason, expiry, and review.
- SDK dependency graphs remain minimal.
- Fixture licenses and sources are recorded.

**In progress — trusted base classifier build (2026-10-22):**
- Added `scripts/dependency_classifier.py`. It selects verified Git blobs for
  explicit base QA locks, support policy and non-test license-helper Go source.
  Only those base sources build the executable; proposed scripts, packages and
  working-tree edits are not used as executable input. The caller must still
  bind this base to the current reviewed PR target, separately from merge base.
- Builds use a private scratch/home/temp directory, exact base-selected compiler,
  serial readonly module builds, disabled cgo/VCS/authentication helpers, fixed
  public proxy/checksum configuration and privately disabled/checked telemetry.
  Module verification must succeed and source/lock bytes must stay unchanged.
  Receipts bind base/tree, source hashes, compiler/target and binary identity,
  omitting source bodies, credentials, filesystem paths and tool diagnostics.
  Classification checks the binary before/after each scan and uses fresh logs.

  Coverage includes base-only selection, substituted bytes, missing/nonregular
  sources, unexpected helper assets, secret/flag removal, compiler/telemetry

Verified incremental pinned Go tool admission on 2026-10-22:

- `scripts/dependency_tool_buildinfo.py` validates checksum-pinned bounded
  Helm/oasdiff archives before writing only the expected binary, exclusively,
  mode 0600 in private scratch. The qualified Go reader inspects it as data;
  downloaded tools are never executed. Binary digests are rechecked after
  inspection. Exported metadata excludes linker flags, VCS details and local
  replacement paths. Unknown versions, replacements, missing dependency sums,
  unsupported platform/CGO and root/version inconsistencies remain blocking.
- `scripts/dependency_tool_sources.py` copies explicit public module ZIPs into
  private scratch and independently recomputes Go `dirhash.Hash1` against the
  binary-recorded `h1`. Only then do bounded root notice bytes reach the shared
  trusted classifier. Exact Go compiler license sources use fresh private
  SumDB-enabled toolchain caches without executing compiler archive binaries.
  Unsafe paths/types, duplicate entries, CRC/DEFLATE failures, mismatched sources
  and per-file/aggregate limits fail with sanitized errors. No proposed module
  code, package lifecycle hooks or upstream dependency graphs are executed.
- `scripts/dependency_tool_admission.py` connects changed generic tool roots to
  exact main-module queries, separate bundled-module and standard-library
  license/advisory decisions, and current-target policy. The parent caller now
  requires tool coverage/verdicts and merges their actually used waiver proofs
  into its final live recheck. Source, image and tool queries share the advisory
  client. Root notices cannot authorize child licenses or hide findings.
- Test-first evidence: seven build-info, eight source-integrity, seven tool
  admission and thirteen caller tests passed. The source DEFLATE error and
  real Helm `v` prefix cases first failed and were fixed. An older caller
  assertion compared assessment dates instead of client arguments; it now

  `evy1407-tool-buildinfo-tests.log`, `evy1407-tool-sources-tests.log`,
  `evy1407-tool-admission-tests.log`, `evy1407-tool-caller-tests.log` and
  `evy1407-tool-admission-docs.log`. Initial red probes are retained.
- Real artifact inspection found 114 Helm and 33 oasdiff Go dependency entries,

  qualification verified all 147 entries plus both compiler sources, with 145

### [x] EVY-1408: Automate changelog, release notes, support windows, and revocation

- Owner: `Codex`
- Priority: `P0`
- Size: `L`
- Depends on: `EVY-005, EVY-705, EVY-1404`

**Initial target files or components:**
- `CHANGELOG.md`
- `scripts/check_release_truth.py`
- `docs/reference/release-notes-template.md`
- `docs/reference/release-revocation.md (new)`
- `.github/workflows/release-artifacts.yml`

**Implementation:**
1. Require user-visible commits or pull requests to carry a changelog fragment or explicit no-changelog label.
2. Generate release notes from reviewed fragments, API changes, migrations, SDK compatibility, security notices, and known limitations.
3. Publish supported versions and security support windows.
4. Define how a compromised, broken, or withdrawn release is marked, announced, and excluded from default install docs.
5. Make missing or contradictory release notes block tagging.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- The changelog cannot say no changes while source is materially ahead.
- Every release documents migrations and compatibility impact.
- Withdrawn releases remain historically visible with a warning.
- Support-window and revocation data are machine readable.

**Required validation:**
- `make changelog-check`
- `python3 scripts/check_release_truth.py`
- `make release-acceptance`

**Expected final commit:**

```text
ci(release): automate history support and revocation (EVY-1408)
```

**Completion evidence (2026-10-21):**

  and `make release-acceptance` passed within `make production-check`.

  selected explicitly from its versioned installation and `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`.
- `make production-check` passed finalize/docs/contracts, acceptance, security
  regressions, lint, gosec, vulnerability analysis, fresh race, live PostgreSQL
  and S3, stress, coverage, migration compatibility, local benchmarks,
  persistence/restart/binary flows and manifest signing/verification smoke.
  No required live dependency check was skipped. Fresh race wiring took
  1113.273s; stress passed 510 executions across 17 cases, 30 each (CPU 1/2/4).
  Coverage met unchanged 80%/81% floors at 86.9% total and 85.7% critical;
  wiring coverage ran fresh in 896.369s. Gosec found zero issues in 1164 files.

  imported-package and five required-module uncalled advisories. A preceding
  run caught GO-2026-6809 in Go certificate verification. Actual Go 1.26.10
  and 1.27.3, locked QA tools and Syft were installed/rebuilt and checked;
  all 16 reviewed native input files remained byte-identical across the
  compiler patch. Scanner whole-tool advisories remain documented.
- `EVYDENCE_RELEASE_ALLOW_LEGACY_EVIDENCE=1 make public-release-verify TAG=v0.1.0-rc.7`
  and `make release-signing-check` passed after production validation. The
  live signing gate includes 31 regressions and checks reviewer/master-only
  environment and publisher-secret custody without retrieving secret values.
- Bounded host RAM6 scheduling used process/PostgreSQL/MinIO caps of 2/3/0.5
  GiB. The unchanged static analyzer ran at 3 GiB only with owned fixtures
  stopped, then the process cap shrank before fixture restart/readiness.
  Four scheduling-safety tests passed. Both owned services were stopped and
  original limits restored after qualification; no container OOM was reported.
- Archive `evy1408-full-qualified-02f74cdc2bc9-112ir_2o`: 201 files /
  354334811 bytes; manifest SHA-256
  `5d3948c3c211811dd2d0d56ef01abc94134be04c430cc15d3f5f3db5371e7a96`.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-150: Documentation, Evaluation, And Adoption Experience

**Objective:** Make the value, limits, installation, API, SDK, architecture, troubleshooting, upgrades, and real-world examples accurate and easy to follow without reading implementation source.

**Score impact:** Documentation, adoption, API design, ecosystem fit, open-source trust, dependency-worthiness, and commercial credibility.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- The value is clear in 30 seconds and a real flow works in five minutes.
- Operator guidance matches tested deployment behavior.
- API and SDK documentation is accurate and versioned.
- Architecture and trust boundaries are understandable.
- Docs are executable, linked, and checked for drift.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-160: Open Source Governance, Scope Discipline, And External Validation

**Objective:** Reduce bus-factor and support risk, verify repository controls, obtain independent security and real-user evidence, and keep the project narrow enough to maintain.

**Score impact:** Open-source trust, ecosystem fit, scope, maintenance maturity, commercial credibility, and final score validity.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- Security and support contacts are durable and operational.
- Review and continuity do not depend silently on one account.
- External security findings are tracked and remediated.
- Real design partners validate the differentiated workflow.
- Peripheral scope is experimental, deprecated, split, or removed.

### [ ] EVY-1608: Create integration boundaries and a standalone offline verifier

5. Clarify commercial licensing and support for server, SDKs, verifier, and custom integrations through a durable contact.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- The verifier works offline from a released package and trust policy.
- Verifier dependency footprint is substantially smaller than the server.
- Integrations use documented stable boundaries.
- Commercial terms do not confuse public AGPL rights or security claims.

## [ ] EPIC EVY-170: Stable Release And Independent 9/10 Exit

**Objective:** Promote only a fully evidenced release candidate, validate it under realistic use, publish a stable supported release, and re-score through independent evidence rather than maintainer intent.

**Score impact:** All dimensions. This is the final gate and must not be used to bypass incomplete technical or external work.

**Epic exit criteria:**
- No open P0 blocker and no unowned P1 blocker remains.
- All required CI, release, compatibility, audit, and pilot evidence is current.
- A reviewed release candidate completes a soak and recovery exercise.
- Stable artifacts, SDKs, images, docs, and support policy agree.
- An independent re-review supports every 9/10 claim.

## External And Maintainer-Owned Completion Rules

The following tickets cannot be completed by repository code alone:

- EVY-1406: GitHub repository and release controls.
- EVY-1601: maintainer-confirmed solo ownership and read-only provider controls;
  a backup maintainer is optional, and private recovery testing is not claimed.
- EVY-1602: verified private security intake and operational ownership.
- EVY-1604: independent security and architecture review.
- EVY-1605: provider-side OpenSSF-style control verification.
- EVY-1606: real design-partner pilot evidence.
- EVY-1703: candidate soak and operator-owned recovery evidence.
- EVY-1704: protected stable publication.
- EVY-1705: independent post-release re-review.

Codex may create scripts, checklists, evidence templates, and remediation
commits for these tickets. It must leave the ticket `[ ]` until the
specified owner supplies verifiable evidence.

## Mandatory Stop Conditions

Stop implementation and leave the current ticket incomplete when:

- a required dependency ticket is incomplete;
- a migration cannot be proven against every supported release state;
- a test is flaky or requires timing increases to pass;
- an external standard or cryptographic behavior is uncertain and no
  authoritative specification or maintained implementation has been
  selected;
- a change would weaken tenant isolation, append-only history, redaction,
  verification, or supported deployment safety;
- a release or security claim would exceed evidence;
- required live PostgreSQL, object-store, provider, signing, or release
  verification cannot be executed;
- external settings or reviews are unavailable.

Record the blocker precisely. Do not guess, silently downgrade the test,
or mark the ticket complete.

## Final 9/10 Checklist

All items below must be true before the scorecard can record 9/10:

- [ ] Stable `v0.1.0` or later is publicly available and verifiable.
- [ ] The stable core API is small, classified, compatibility-gated, and
  fully covered by published SDKs.
- [ ] No known metadata-only operation is labeled cryptographically
  verified.
- [ ] All production writes are database-authoritative and failure
  atomic.
- [ ] Idempotency and audit sequencing are concurrency safe.
- [ ] Object and database state use staged finalization, reconciliation,
  and paired recovery.
- [ ] Supported SBOM, VEX, scanner, DSSE, and Sigstore inputs pass the
  published conformance matrix.
- [ ] Real cryptographic verification is bound to explicit trust policy.
- [ ] The production god aggregate and global request lock are removed.
- [ ] Critical behavior has unit, integration, race, fuzz, stress,
  failure-injection, upgrade, and black-box release evidence.
- [ ] Readiness, metrics, logs, traces, migrations, workers, Helm, and
  runbooks match tested behavior.
- [ ] Build, container, SDK, SBOM, provenance, signatures, changelog, and
  release truth point to the same source.
- [ ] Branch, review, security scanning, secret scanning, release, and
  vulnerability-reporting controls are verified.
- [ ] An independent security review has no unresolved critical or high
  finding.
- [ ] At least two external design partners have completed the core
  workflow with sanitized evidence.
- [ ] Maintainer continuity, security response, support windows, and
  patch release processes are operational.
- [ ] An independent re-review supports every score with evidence.

Until every applicable item is true, keep the public project status and
numeric score conservative.
